Their stateful exercises are\\r\\n// mounted by React; the former reader-page renderer is never invoked here.\\r\\nfunction sections(children: ReactNode): ContentSection[] {\\r\\n  return Children.toArray(children).flatMap(child => {\\r\\n    if (!isValidElement<ContentSection[\'props\']>(child)) return [];\\r\\n    return child.type === Fragment ? sections(child.props.children) : [child];\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction reference(number: number): ContentSection {\\r\\n  const section = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(item => item.props.number === number);\\r\\n  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional reference section ${number}`);\\r\\n  return section;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction groupSections(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\') {\\r\\n  return sections(GroupLessons({ id: group }).props.children);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction groupSection(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\', title: string) {\\r\\n  const section = groupSections(group).find(item => item.props.title === title);\\r\\n  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional section: ${title}`);\\r\\n  return cloneElement(section, { id: `course-${group}-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \'-\')}` });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction NoteSection({ title, children }: { title: string; children: ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"co-section\\"><div className=\\"co-section-heading\\"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ReferenceBody({ number, omitTransition = false }: { number: number; omitTransition?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const source = reference(number);\\r\\n  const children = Children.toArray(source.props.children);\\r\\n  // These source sections end in navigation prose for the former ordering.\\r\\n  // The course shell now owns transitions; the teaching stays in its source.\\r\\n  return <section className={`co-section cr-course-section ${source.props.dark ? \'cr-dark\' : \'\'}`} data-reference-source={number}>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-section-heading\\"><h2>{source.props.title}</h2></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cr-step-body\\">{omitTransition ? children.slice(0, -1) : children}</div>\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction FamilySupplement({ group }: { group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\' }) {\\r\\n  const source = reference(group === \'group-1\' ? 3 : 5);\\r\\n  const family = sections(source.props.children).find(child => child.type === WordFamily);\\r\\n  if (!family) throw new Error(`Missing approved ${group} supplementary family`);\\r\\n  return <details className=\\"co-practical-note\\"><summary>More expressions for the same relationship</summary>\\r\\n    <p>Use the stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. These sentences preserve that connection. They do not say every person with a key borrows a locker.</p>\\r\\n    {family}\\r\\n  </details>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction IndicatorNote({ group }: { group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\' }) {\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const id = useId();\\r\\n  const content = groupSections(group);\\r\\n  const expressions = content.slice(2, 9);\\r\\n  // Two introductions and the seven complete expression explanations belong\\r\\n  // together in one concept note, not nine separate navigation destinations.\\r\\n  return <>{content.slice(0, 2)}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-word-tabs\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Choose an expression\\">{expressions.map((section, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={section.props.title} aria-pressed={selected === index} aria-controls={`${id}-${index}`} onClick={() => setSelected(index)}>{section.props.title?.split(\':\')[0]}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    {expressions.map((section, index) => <div key={section.props.title} id={`${id}-${index}`} hidden={selected !== index}>{section}</div>)}\\r\\n    <FamilySupplement group={group}/>\\r\\n    <NoteSection title=\\"Recall the expressions\\"><p>Explain the relationship before turning each card. Compare the complete ideas and the direction with your answer.</p><StudyDeck group={group}/></NoteSection>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction FoundationPractice() {\\r\\n  const definitionQuestions = [1, 2, 4].flatMap(number => sections(reference(number).props.children).filter(child => child.type === ReferenceQuestion).map((child, index) => cloneElement(child, { key: `${number}-${index}` })));\\r\\n  const fresh = sections(Fundamentals().props.children).find(section => section.props.title === \'Enough does not mean the only way\');\\r\\n  if (!fresh) throw new Error(\'Missing approved fresh sufficient-condition check\');\\r\\n  const freshContent = Children.toArray(fresh.props.children);\\r\\n  const freshCheck = freshContent[freshContent.length - 1];\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <NoteSection title=\\"Separate the rule from the fact\\"><blockquote>If you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.</blockquote><p>Use the rule to decide what each fact establishes. A wrong answer is a reason to inspect the connection and try again.</p>{definitionQuestions}</NoteSection>\\r\\n    <NoteSection title=\\"Try a fresh case\\">{freshCheck}</NoteSection>\\r\\n    <NoteSection title=\\"Test the limit with a possible case\\"><ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Someone claims that getting a bicycle proves you won the raffle. Which possible case fits our rule but disproves that claim?\\" options={[\'You win and get a bicycle.\', \'You do not win, but get a bicycle as a gift.\', \'You win and never get a bicycle.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'This fits both the rule and the claim. It does not show why the claim adds something.\', \'The rule promises a bicycle to a winner. It does not exclude a bicycle received as a gift without winning, so this case fits the rule and disproves the claim.\', \'Winning without getting a bicycle breaks the original rule. We need a case that keeps the rule true.\']}/></NoteSection>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction GroupPractice({ group }: { group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\' }) {\\r\\n  const meaningCheck = groupSection(group, \'Check the meaning, not just the word\');\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    {groupSection(group, group === \'group-1\' ? \'Find the target, then place it\' : \'Find the requirement, then place it\')}\\r\\n    {groupSection(group, \'From sentence to diagram\')}\\r\\n    {group === \'group-1\' && groupSection(group, \'Read the only as a complete expression\')}\\r\\n    {cloneElement(meaningCheck, {}, Children.toArray(meaningCheck.props.children).slice(0, -1))}\\r\\n    <details className=\\"co-practical-note\\"><summary>More sentences to recall and explain</summary>{groupSection(group, \'Say the rule without the clues\')}</details>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction PermissionBoundary() {\\r\\n  const boundary = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(section => section.props[\'data-reference-boundary\'] !== undefined);\\r\\n  if (!boundary) throw new Error(\'Missing approved permission and instruction boundary\');\\r\\n  return <NoteSection title=\\"Keep permission and instructions distinct\\"><p>A conditional can give permission or an instruction. Preserve that strength instead of turning it into a report of what someone did.</p>{Children.toArray(boundary.props.children).slice(3)}</NoteSection>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ContrapositivePractice() {\\r\\n  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState<boolean[]>(() => referencePracticeItems.map(() => false));\\r\\n  return <NoteSection title=\\"Build an original and its contrapositive\\">\\r\\n    <p>For each sentence, identify the indicator and both complete conditions. Choose the original diagram, then its contrapositive. Read both back before checking. If an answer needs revision, inspect the direction, qualifications and negation, then retry.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\" role=\\"status\\">Sentence {current + 1} of {referencePracticeItems.length}</p>\\r\\n    {referencePracticeItems.map((item, index) => <div key={item.sentence} hidden={current !== index} data-course-practice-item={index + 1}><IntegratedItem item={item} number={index + 1} onChecked={value => setChecked(previous => previous.map((old, itemIndex) => itemIndex === index ? value : old))}/></div>)}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => setCurrent(current - 1)}>Previous sentence</button><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === referencePracticeItems.length - 1 || !checked[current]} onClick={() => setCurrent(current + 1)}>Next sentence</button></div>\\r\\n    <p>Moving between sentences keeps your choices and feedback.</p>\\r\\n  </NoteSection>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Content({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  switch (id) {\\r\\n    case \'meaning\': return <ReferenceBody number={1} omitTransition/>;\\r\\n    case \'sufficient\': return <ReferenceBody number={2} omitTransition/>;\\r\\n    case \'necessary\': return <><ReferenceBody number={4} omitTransition/><ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"You get a bicycle. Does that establish that you won the raffle?\\" options={[\'Yes. Getting a bicycle proves winning.\', \'No. You could get a bicycle without winning.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You made the required result enough to establish winning. The rule does not say winning is the only way to get a bicycle.\', \'You could buy a bicycle or receive one as a gift. Neither possibility breaks the rule, which only tells us what must follow if you win.\']}/></>;\\r\\n    case \'must-arrow\': return <><ReferenceBody number={8}/><NoteSection title=\\"Recall enough and required\\"><p>Use these new examples to check the roles and their direction.</p><StudyDeck group=\\"foundation\\"/></NoteSection></>;\\r\\n    case \'sufficient-words\': return <IndicatorNote group=\\"group-1\\"/>;\\r\\n    case \'necessary-words\': return <><IndicatorNote group=\\"group-2\\"/><PermissionBoundary/></>;\\r\\n    case \'whole-meaning\': return <><ReferenceBody number={11} omitTransition/>{groupSection(\'group-1\', \'Keep the whole condition\')}{groupSection(\'group-2\', \'Keep the whole condition\')}{groupSection(\'group-2\', \'Compare only with the only\')}<ReferenceBody number={12} omitTransition/></>;\\r\\n    case \'missing-requirement\': return <><ReferenceBody number={6}/><ReferenceBody number={7} omitTransition/></>;\\r\\n    case \'contrapositive\': return <><ReferenceBody number={9}/><details className=\\"co-practical-note\\"><summary>Practical questions about diagramming</summary><ReferenceBody number={10} omitTransition/></details><NoteSection title=\\"Two possible rooms, one limit\\"><NegativeCaseLab/></NoteSection></>;\\r\\n    case \'foundation-practice\': return <FoundationPractice/>;\\r\\n    case \'sufficient-practice\': return <><GroupPractice group=\\"group-1\\"/><NoteSection title=\\"Build a case that proves the limit\\"><CounterexampleLab/></NoteSection></>;\\r\\n    case \'mixed-practice\': return <><GroupPractice group=\\"group-2\\"/><NoteSection title=\\"Keep the rule while the wording changes\\"><RuleComparisonLab/></NoteSection></>;\\r\\n    case \'contrapositive-practice\': return <ContrapositivePractice/>;\\r\\n    default: throw new Error(`Unknown opening course content: ${id}`);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Article body only. Course routes own titles, navigation and completion. */\\r\\nexport function OpeningCourseContent({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-opening cr-sequence opening-course-content\\"><Content key={id} id={id}/></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nName\\r\\n----\\r\\nOpeningCourse.test.tsx\\r\\nOpeningCourse.tsx\\r\\nOpeningCourseContent.tsx\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

findTheConclusion from \\"./find-the-conclusion.json\\";\\r\\nimport rebuttalRephrase from \\"./rebuttal-rephrase.json\\";\\r\\nimport spotTheCallback from \\"./spot-the-callback.json\\";\\r\\nimport spotTheRelationship from \\"./spot-the-relationship.json\\";\\r\\nimport vocabRehab from \\"./vocab-rehab.json\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/** An Activity definition as authored, before it exists as a row. */\\r\\nexport interface SeedActivity {\\r\\n  slug: string;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  subtitle: string;\\r\\n  item_format: ItemFormat;\\r\\n  instructions: ActivityInstructions;\\r\\n  items: ActivityItem[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Section mode only, and absent on every Activity authored before it. A\\r\\n   * section carries its own clock and names the captured section it came from;\\r\\n   * the instructions block stays empty in the JSON and is carried from the base\\r\\n   * Activity at seed time, so the rules are authored once.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  mode?: ActivityMode;\\r\\n  time_goal_minutes?: TimeGoalMinutes;\\r\\n  source_section?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Ordered by the scope of text the skill operates on, word up to passage, which\\r\\n * is both the teaching progression and the order the teacher library lists\\r\\n * them. Negation Station is not here: it predates this pipeline, its items are\\r\\n * authored verbatim, and it is deliberately left alone.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport const SEED_ACTIVITIES: SeedActivity[] = [\\r\\n  vocabRehab as SeedActivity, // a word\\r\\n  spotTheRelationship as SeedActivity, // a sentence\\r\\n  conditionalRephrase as SeedActivity, // a statement, translated\\r\\n  findTheConclusion as SeedActivity, // an argument\\r\\n  rebuttalRephrase as SeedActivity, // an argument, restated\\r\\n  dumbItDown as SeedActivity, // a paragraph\\r\\n  spotTheCallback as SeedActivity, // a passage\\r\\n  // The captured sections. Each is a whole LR section run unbroken against the\\r\\n  // clock, and they are last for a reason that is not length: everything above\\r\\n  // is ordered by the scope of text a skill works on, and this is a different\\r\\n  // axis, a section worked out loud from memory rather than analysed on the\\r\\n  // page. Ordered by PrepTest so the list does not reshuffle when the next one\\r\\n  // is built.\\r\\n  //\\r\\n  // The door they are reached through, `read-recall-colour-sections`, is not\\r\\n  // seeded from here. It carries no items of its own -- its content is the list\\r\\n  // of these -- so it is created by its own migration and appears in\\r\\n  // ACTIVITY_LIBRARY_ORDER below without a row in this array.\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt1S3 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt21S3 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt101S2 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt102S3 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt103S1 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt104S1 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt105S1 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt105S2 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt106S2 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt112S4 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt134S3 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n  readRecallColourPt140S1 as unknown as SeedActivity,\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const SEED_ACTIVITY_SLUGS = SEED_ACTIVITIES.map((a) => a.slug);\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The order the Activity library lists every Activity in, teaching progression\\r\\n * first: the scope of text the skill operates on, a word up to a passage.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * This is separate from SEED_ACTIVITIES on purpose. That array is the seed\\r\\n * pipeline\'s input and must stay exactly the Activities the migration\\r\\n * inserts. This one is the display order and includes Negation Station, which\\r\\n * predates the pipeline but still has to sit somewhere sensible in the list.\\r\\n * It goes with the other statement-level work, beside Conditional Rephrase:\\r\\n * one turns a statement around, the other negates it.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Without this the library sorted by created_at, so it read newest-seeded\\r\\n * first and the Activities inserted by one migration tied arbitrarily among\\r\\n * themselves. An Activity not named here still appears, after these, so adding\\r\\n * one by hand never makes it vanish from the library.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport const ACTIVITY_LIBRARY_ORDER = [\\r\\n  \\"vocab-rehab\\", // a word\\r\\n  \\"spot-the-relationship\\", // a sentence\\r\\n  \\"conditional-rephrase\\", // a statement, translated\\r\\n  \\"negation-station\\", // a statement, negated\\r\\n  \\"find-the-conclusion\\", // an argument\\r\\n  \\"rebuttal-rephrase\\", // an argument, restated\\r\\n  \\"dumb-it-down\\", // a paragraph\\r\\n  \\"spot-the-callback\\", // a passage\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-sections\\", // the door: a whole section, out loud\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt1-s3\\", // a whole section, against the clock\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt21-s3\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt101-s2\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt102-s3\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt103-s1\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt104-s1\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt105-s1\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt105-s2\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt106-s2\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt112-s4\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt134-s3\\",\\r\\n  \\"read-recall-colour-pt140-s1\\",\\r\\n] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Where an Activity sits in the library. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"3ae093","wall_time_seconds":2.0490196,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3142,"output":"INSTRUCTIONS {\\"coach\\": {\\"drill\\": \\"a conditional-translation drill: the student rewrites a statement as If [sufficient], then [necessary] and gives its contrapositive\\", \\"rubric\\": [\\"Two forms are credited and they are the same statement: the if-then form, and its contrapositive. A student who gives either one has translated correctly. On an ordinary one-way conditional, nothing else counts as a direction: the converse (swapping the two sides) and the inverse (negating both without swapping) are errors, not alternative phrasings. Biconditional items are the stated exception, covered below.\\", \\"The core judgment is which condition is sufficient and which is necessary. Words like if, when, whenever, every, all and the only mark the sufficient side. Words like only if, only when, unless, without, except, requires, presupposes, depends on, necessitates and must mark the necessary side.\\", \\"Compound conditions (both X and Y) and internal contrasts (X rather than Y) must be carried into the answer whole, not simplified away.\\", \\"The compound flip is a correctness rule, not a stylistic one. Contraposing \\\\\\"If A and B, then C\\\\\\" yields \\\\\\"If not C, then not A or not B\\\\\\". A student who carries the and through unchanged has written a stronger claim than the statement supports, and that is an error to name, not a phrasing quibble. The same applies in reverse: an or on the trigger side becomes an and.\\", \\"Two errors have names worth using in the feedback, and both apply to one-way conditionals, not to biconditionals. Reversing the arrow is treating the necessary condition as if meeting it guaranteed the trigger. Negating the trigger is concluding that because the trigger did not fire, the outcome cannot happen. Say which one the student made.\\", \\"Unless, until and without translate as if not on the trigger side. When the idea being negated there is itself a compound, the and or or inside it flips too.\\", \\"With no and none the negation attaches to the necessary side, never to the trigger. The subject of the sentence still goes on the if side, intact.\\", \\"If and only if and then and only then are biconditionals, where the arrow genuinely runs both ways and the converse is therefore credited. Only on these items. A student may write both conditionals, or a single two-way statement, or say that the two sides stand or fall together. Do not credit an exclusive either-or, which says something different. Otherwise is not a biconditional; it means if the first alternative does not happen, the stated consequence follows.\\", \\"Wording is free. Judge the logical structure, not the phrasing.\\"], \\"partial_rule\\": \\"Mark \'partial\' when the student produces a well-formed conditional but reverses sufficiency and necessity, drops a negation, or trims a compound or contrasting condition down to one part. That is the whole game.\\", \\"If, when, whenever, every, all, the only: these mark the sufficient side, the trigger.\\", \\"Only if, only when, unless, without, requires, depends on, must: these mark the necessary side, what is required.\\", \\"A necessary condition is required but never a guarantee. That keeps\\r\\n// the wings honest (nothing generated, nothing paraphrased) and it means a\\r\\n// student\'s wing reps and their yard reps are about the same material.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// Selection is deterministic. Rounds are picked by offset, not at random, so a\\r\\n// round is reproducible, testable, and reviewable before a student sees it.\\r\\n\\r\\nimport type { ActivityItem } from \\"../../../api/types\\";\\r\\nimport { indicatorOf, parseRouting } from \\"../routing\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface RoundItem {\\r\\n  order: number;\\r\\n  statement: string;\\r\\n  /** The sufficient clause: the one that belongs on the IF track. */\\r\\n  ifText: string;\\r\\n  /** The necessary clause: the one that belongs on the THEN track. */\\r\\n  thenText: string;\\r\\n  /** The indicator the item turns on, when the bank names one. */\\r\\n  indicator?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Whether to present the necessary clause first. Alternating by item order\\r\\n   * keeps the correct answer off a fixed position without reaching for\\r\\n   * randomness, which would make a round impossible to reproduce.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  swapped: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Every bank item whose credited answer parses into two clauses. */\\r\\nexport function routableItems(items: ActivityItem[]): RoundItem[] {\\r\\n  const out: RoundItem[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const item of items) {\\r\\n    const routing = parseRouting(item.credited_answers[0]);\\r\\n    if (!routing) continue;\\r\\n    out.push({\\r\\n      order: item.order,\\r\\n      statement: item.prompt,\\r\\n      ifText: routing.ifClause.text,\\r\\n      thenText: routing.thenClause.text,\\r\\n      indicator: indicatorOf(item.explanation),\\r\\n      swapped: item.order % 2 === 0,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The Sorting Yard\'s round: ten statements, taken in order from an offset. */\\r\\nexport function buildSortingRound(items: ActivityItem[], size = 10, offset = 0): RoundItem[] {\\r\\n  const pool = routableItems(items);\\r\\n  if (!pool.length) return [];\\r\\n  const start = ((offset % pool.length) + pool.length) % pool.length;\\r\\n  return Array.from({ length: Math.min(size, pool.length) }, (_, i) => pool[(start + i) % pool.length]);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The hard indicator lexicon, in the spec\'s order. The look-alike pair sits\\r\\n * adjacent on purpose: \\"only if\\" marks the necessary condition and \\"the only\\"\\r\\n * marks the sufficient one, and drilling them apart is how that gets confused.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport const LEXICON = [\\r\\n  \\"unless\\",\\r\\n  \\"without\\",\\r\\n  \\"except\\",\\r\\n  \\"only if\\",\\r\\n  \\"the only\\",\\r\\n  \\"presupposes\\",\\r\\n  \\"depends on\\",\\r\\n  \\"necessitates\\",\\r\\n  \\"requires\\",\\r\\n] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type LeverWord = (typeof LEXICON)[number];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface Lever {\\r\\n  word: LeverWord;\\r\\n  /** Real bank items that turn on this indicator. A lever with none is not rendered. */\\r\\n  prompts: RoundItem[];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Group the bank behind the levers. An indicator matches a lever when the\\r\\n * bank\'s named indicator contains the lever\'s word, which catches the authored\\r\\n * variants (\\"except where\\", \\"no…without\\", \\"not…unless\\") without a second list\\r\\n * to keep in sync.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function buildGallery(items: ActivityItem[]): Lever[] {\\r\\n  const pool = routableItems(items);\\r\\n  return LEXICON.map((word) => ({\\r\\n    word,\\r\\n    prompts: pool.filter((p) => {\\r\\n      const ind = p.indicator?.toLowerCase();\\r\\n      if (!ind) return false;\\r\\n      // \\"only if\\" must not swallow the bare \\"only\\" items, and \\"the only\\" must\\r\\n      // not be collected by the \\"only if\\" lever. The existing records belong to different systems:

- General Activities use assigned routes `/activities/:assignmentId`, teacher preview `/activities/preview/:activityId`, `activity_item_responses` and `activity_assignments`. Evidence: `src/App.tsx:534`, `:551`; `api/activitiesClient.ts:219`, `:270`. Actual activity definitions come from the **database**, not the checked-in seed JSON: `content/index.ts:1`. I did not verify the live database.
- Conditional Practice uses account-scoped snapshots/history in `conditional_practice_runs`, with local caching and revision handling: `conditionals/practiceStore.ts:34`, `:59`, `:129`. Its General tile resolves an existing assignment, while specialist modes offer student-selectable variables/words filters: `ConditionalPractice.tsx:25`, `:108`, `:182`.
- Refresher progress is browser localStorage under a shared key, not account-scoped assignment evidence: `ConditionalIndicatorsRefresher.tsx:13`, `:22`. It cannot currently substantiate teacher-assigned completion.
- General timing is fixed by question position: first12 untimed, next25 at45 seconds, remainder30 seconds: `conditionals/generalPhases.ts:1`. Importing this flow unchanged would conflict with teacher-controlled timing.

**Current bootcamp integration is narrower than the resource names suggest.**

`src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/index.ts:29` defines links to the refresher and Conditional Practice room, but a complete `src` reference search found **no consumer of `conditionalResources`**. These are dormant resource definitions, not confirmed rendered links.

The opening currently imports its own approved teaching/check components, labs, mixed-family practice and StudyDeck: `components/OpeningCourseContent.tsx:1`. It does **not** import the Activity Centre engines or question banks. It shares the refresher’s GROUPS vocabulary through `ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:7`.

The bootcamp’s `/activities/:unitId` is its own guided `QuestionRunner`, not the Activity Centre assignment route: `routes.tsx:119`, `:233`. Recorded skill drills and simulator also use that runner and bootcamp course state: `routes.tsx:264`, `:275`; `useConditionalProgress.ts:6`. The four opening labs are instructional practice with feedback/retry. They should not be described as a separate independent final assessment; I found no distinct final-assessment route in the current route inventory.

Concrete reuse path:

1. Adapt SortingYard for complete-condition placement after arrow direction.
2. Adapt diagram building with the seven selected refresher items after Groups1/2; introduce contrapositive answers only after its lesson.
3. Use reviewed Group1/2 TriggerGallery rounds for necessary-condition identification.
4. Keep final independent assessment separate, using fresh reviewed questions and explicit attempt evidence.
5. Preserve existing assignment IDs, recorded responses and histories. Connect reusable interactions through an adapter to the intended bootcamp contract.

Full import also carries copy defects: seed guidance groups “unless/without/except” with ordinary necessary indicators, says a necessary condition is “never a guarantee,” and says reversing is “always wrong” despite later biconditionals (`conditional-rephrase.json:11`, `:28`, `:33`). Shared engines plus a curated manifest avoids importing those instructions and later prerequisites.

No files changed, no backend calls, no tests run for this read-only audit.
